Original requirements are retained verbatim below. They remain normative except for individually quoted, explicitly scoped amendments (including labeled example errata) in the decisions that follow. A summary, omission, finite-model example, or conflicting added sentence does not implicitly override an original requirement.

<!-- original-requirements:start -->
Develop a System Verilog RTL module `vga_controller` that takes a 25 MHz clock signal, a reset signal, and an 8-bit color input as inputs, and generates the necessary VGA synchronization and color signals to drive a VGA-compatible display. The design should follow the VGA timing protocol for a 640x480 resolution.

### Design Specification :
The `vga_controller` module implements a VGA signal generation mechanism to output pixel color data and synchronize signals, controlling the display of pixels on the screen according to the VGA timing standard. The controller should divide each frame into defined intervals and maintain precise horizontal and vertical timing cycles to manage pixel rendering and screen refresh.

###  Interface :

#### Inputs :
  - `clock`(1-bit): 25 MHz clock signal to control the VGA timings.
  - `reset`(1-bit): An ACTIVE-HIGH  asynchronous signal to initialize the VGA controller. When HIGH, the module performs the 
  following:
        -  Initializes the horizontal and vertical FSMs to their ACTIVE state.
        -  Resets the 10-bit counters (h_counter and v_counter) to zero.
        -  Sets the 1-bit output signal line_done to LOW.
  - `color_in`([7:0], 8-bit): Pixel color data in `RRRGGGBB` format, where `color_in` [7:5] represents `red`, `color_in`[4:2] represents `green`, and `color_in`[1:0] represents blue intensities.

#### Outputs :
  - `hsync`(1-bit): An ACTIVE-LOW pulse indicates horizontal synchronization; hsync is HIGH outside H_PULSE.
  - `vsync`(1-bit): An ACTIVE-LOW pulse indicates vertical synchronization; vsync is HIGH outside V_PULSE.
  - `red`, `green`, `blue` ([7:0],8-bit each):  RGB color signals, used to control pixel color output to the VGA DAC (Digital-to-Analog Converter)
  - `next_x`, `next_y` ([9:0], 10-bit each): Current pixel coordinates within the active display area
  - `sync`(1-bit): ACTIVE HIGH Sync signal for VGA; should be held constant at 0.
  - `clk` (1-bit): VGA clock, directly connected to the input clock.
  - `blank`(1-bit): Legacy sync qualifier, defined exactly as hsync AND vsync. It is HIGH outside sync pulses and is not an indicator of the complete pixel blanking interval.
  
### Features :
  - `640x480 Resolution`: Support VGA display timing for a resolution of 640x480. 
  - `Sync Signal Generation`: Generate `hsync` and `vsync` signals appropriately for VGA display synchronization. 
  - `Pixel Position Tracking`: Track the current pixel position in terms of `next_x` and `next_y` for each frame, wrapping to zero outside of the active display area.
  - `Color Output Control`: Display the color values (red, green, blue) when within the active display region; output zero for blanking periods.
    
### VGA Timing Protocol for a 640 x 480 resolution :
- Step 1: The VGA pixel clock runs at 25 MHz
- Step 2: Both `hsync` and `vsync` start in active mode (logic level HIGH).
- Step 3: `hsync` remains HIGH while displaying 640 pixels across one row(640 cycles)
- Step 4: For each of the 640 clock cycles, the voltages on the `red`, `green`, and `blue` output lines generate an 8-bit value corresponding to their intensities. These intensities are converted into a voltage ranging between 0 and 0.7 V by three separate Digital to Analog Converter (DAC) units provided externally. The DAC implementation is not included in the design.
- Step 5: After 640 clock cycles, the `red`, `green`, and `blue` output lines are set to 0000_0000 and `hsync` remains HIGH for additional 16-pixel clocks. This period is referred as FRONTPORCH.
- Step 6: After the 16-pixel clocks, `hsync` is set to logic-level LOW for 96-pixel clocks. This period is referred as HORIZONTAL SYNC PULSE period
- Step 7: After the 96-pixel clocks `hsync` is set to logic-level HIGH through for 48-pixel clocks. This period is referred as BACKPORCH.
- Step 8: `hsync` then returns to the start of active mode (step 2, above), and the process is repeated for the next row of pixels. Each row of pixels is a line.
- Step 9: `vsync` remains in active mode (logic level HIGH) for 480 lines.
- Step 10: After 480 lines, the voltages on the `red`, `green`, and `blue` output lines are set to 0, and the VSYNC line remains high through its frontporch (10 lines)
- Step 11: `vsync` is set to logic-level LOW for 2 lines (this is the vertical sync pulse).
- Step 12: `vsync` is set to logic-level HIGH through its backporch (33 lines).
- Step 13: `vsync` then returns to the start of active mode (step 2, above), and the process is repeated for the next frame.

### Parameters :
  #### Horizontal Timing:
  - `H_ACTIVE`: Number of cycles in the active display period (fixed at 640).
  - `H_FRONT`: Number of cycles in the front porch period (fixed at 16).
  - `H_PULSE`: Number of cycles for the horizontal sync pulse (fixed at 96).
  - `H_BACK`: Number of cycles for the back porch period (fixed at 48)
  
  #### Vertical Timing:
   - `V_ACTIVE`: Number of lines in the active display area (fixed at 480).
   - `V_FRONT`: Number of lines in the vertical front porch period (fixed at 10).
   - `V_PULSE`: Number of lines in the vertical sync pulse (fixed at 2).
   - `V_BACK`: Number of lines in the vertical back porch period (fixed at 33)
   
## `1. State Tracking`:
Two state machines govern the horizontal and vertical cycles:
Horizontal State Machine: Transitions through the active, front porch, pulse, and back porch states.
Vertical State Machine: Transitions similarly but advances one line for each completed horizontal frame.

## 2. Counters:
`h_counter ([9:0], 10-bit)`: Tracks the current pixel position within a horizontal line.
`v_counter ([9:0], 10-bit)`: Tracks the current line position within a frame.

## `3. Sync Signals`:
`hsync` and `vsync`: Generated based on the horizontal and vertical state machines, respectively, with hsync/vsync LOW during their corresponding pulse periods and HIGH otherwise. The textual timing steps are normative; the illustrative wavedrom may not override these polarity or phase rules.

## `4. Color Control Signals`:
red, green, blue Registers: Hold pixel color data based on color_in during the active display period.
Outside the active period, these registers are cleared to ensure blanking.

## `Functional Behavior`:
### `1. Reset Behavior`:
During Reset (reset = 1):
- 10-bit counters `hcounter` and `vcounter` are set to zero
- Horizontal FSM is set to the `H_ACTIVE` state and Vertical FSM is set to the `V_ACTIVE` state
- `line_done` is set to logic LOW.

### `2. Horizontal and Vertical State Machine Operation`:
The horizontal state machine increments `h_counter` and transitions between states (active, front porch, sync pulse, back porch) based on predefined timing parameters.
The vertical state machine increments `v_counter` and transitions similarly, but only when the horizontal machine signals the end of a line.

### `3. Pixel Position Tracking:`
`next_x` and `next_y` track the upcoming pixel's coordinates during the active display period. These are reset outside the active region.

### `4. RGB Signal Assignment`:
Active Period: RGB outputs are derived from `color_in`, scaled to 8 bits.
 -  `red`     = {`color_in[7:5]`, 5'd0};
 -  `green` = {`color_in[4:2]`, 5'd0};
 -  `blue`   = {`color_in[1:0]`, 6'd0};
Blanking Period: RGB outputs are set to zero.

### `5. Display Control`:
blank = hsync AND vsync. RGB outputs are zero unless both horizontal and vertical FSMs are ACTIVE; blank is a separate legacy sync qualifier.
sync Signal: Fixed to LOW in this design.

## Key Notes:
- All logic is implemented within a single always_ff block, without explicitly separating combinational and sequential logic.
- State transitions and counter updates are triggered on the positive edge of the clock.
- The module ensures proper synchronization and blanking for a VGA display with a resolution of 640x480 pixels at 25 MHz clock input.
 
### `Sample Signal Waveform` :
```wavedrom
{
  "signal": [
    {"name": "clock", "wave": "p.|.|.|.|.|.|.|.|.", "data": ["Clock"]},
    {"name": "reset", "wave": "1.0...............", "data": ["Reset"]},
    {
      "name": "h_counter",
      "wave": "3.45678999.9999999",
      "data": [
        "0 (Active Start)",
        "1",
        "2",
        "3",
        "...",
        "639 (Active End)",
        "0 (Front Porch Start)",
        "...",
        "15 (Front Porch End)",
        "0 (Sync Pulse Start)",
        "...",
        "95 (Sync Pulse End)",
        "0 (Back Porch Start)",
        "...",
        "47 (Back Porch End)",
        "0 (Active Start)"
      ]
    },
    {"name": "line_done", "wave": "3................5", "data": ["LOW (Start)", "HIGH (Back Porch End)"]},
    {"name": "hsync", "wave": "0.1........0..1..0", "data": ["HIGH (Active)", "LOW (Sync Pulse)"]},
    {
      "name": "v_counter",
      "wave": "3...............45",
      "data": [
        "0 (Initial)",
        "0 (No Increment )",
        "1 (Row Complete)"
      ]
    },
    {"name": "vsync", "wave": "0.................", "data": ["LOW (Inactive)", "LOW (No Sync Pulse)"]},
    {"name": "blank", "wave": "0.................", "data": ["LOW (Inactive)", "LOW (No Sync Pulse)"]}
  ],
  "foot": {
    "text": "This shows the interaction between the Horizontal and Vertical FSMs. The Vertical FSM increments v_counter when the Horizontal FSM signals line_done at the end of a row."
  },
  "config": {
    "hscale": 4
  }
}
```

Local experimental completion of VGA edge/phase laws. These exact rules supersede earlier unspecified counter/publication timing, ambiguous "upcoming" coordinates, waveform drawings and reset-output/architecture shorthand. They are declared benchmark choices, not recovered designer intent:
- The eight listed timing constants are fixed to their stated values. A line has exactly 800 pixel clocks and a frame exactly 525 lines. Reset or local experiment initialization sets the horizontal/vertical position to zero and both phases ACTIVE. No pixel is consumed on reset release; the first subsequent rising input-clock edge publishes pixel (0,0).
- At each rising clock edge with reset=0, publish all registered display outputs using the PRE-EDGE horizontal position h in 0..799, vertical line v in 0..524, and current color_in. Then advance h by one modulo 800. On the edge consuming h=799, advance v by one modulo 525 on that same edge, with no extra registered-line_done delay. Counter/FSM representation may differ internally but must implement these observable positions and phase lengths.
- The published hsync is zero exactly for 656<=h<752, otherwise one. The published vsync is zero exactly for 490<=v<492, otherwise one. Published blank is the AND of those same newly published sync values, not their preceding registered values. sync is always zero.
- If h<640 and v<480, publish next_x=h,next_y=v and red={color_in[7:5],5'b0},green={color_in[4:2],5'b0},blue={color_in[1:0],6'b0}. Otherwise publish all five coordinate/color outputs zero. Coordinates accompany the just-published pixel; the word "next" does not introduce an extra increment or pipeline stage.
- RGB, coordinates, hsync, vsync and blank are registered and retain their published values between rising edges, including when color_in changes. On asynchronous reset assertion, immediately set RGB and coordinates to zero, hsync=vsync=blank=1,sync=0, positions zero and internal line_done=0. Reset has priority over any simultaneous clock edge; while reset stays high these values hold. Reset release retains them until the next rising edge.
- clk is a physical continuous copy of clock at both edges and during reset, independent of registered display outputs. This direct connection is the explicit exception to the earlier "all logic in one always_ff" shorthand; all registered display/state updates use rising edges plus asynchronous reset. line_done is internal line-completion bookkeeping, not an additional public port; the enumerated public output list is authoritative.


This is a locally revised benchmark prompt (2026-10-01), with explicit errata that supersede conflicting earlier clauses. It is not an unchanged upstream CVDP problem. Implementation choices for behavior still unspecified must be disclosed and must not contradict these requirements.

<!-- original-requirements:end -->

Decisions and verification instance

Original implementation instruction (verbatim):

Develop a System Verilog RTL module `vga_controller` that takes a 25 MHz clock signal, a reset signal, and an 8-bit color input as inputs, and generates the necessary VGA synchronization and color signals to drive a VGA-compatible display. The design should follow the VGA timing protocol for a 640x480 resolution.

Named module interface binding

Module: vga_controller.
Connect ports by name. The enumeration below does not establish positional order.
- clock: input; 1 bits in the concrete formal instance.
- reset: input; 1 bits in the concrete formal instance.
- color_in: input; 8 bits in the concrete formal instance.
- hsync: output; 1 bits in the concrete formal instance.
- vsync: output; 1 bits in the concrete formal instance.
- red: output; 8 bits in the concrete formal instance.
- green: output; 8 bits in the concrete formal instance.
- blue: output; 8 bits in the concrete formal instance.
- next_x: output; 10 bits in the concrete formal instance.
- next_y: output; 10 bits in the concrete formal instance.
- sync: output; 1 bits in the concrete formal instance.
- clk: output; 1 bits in the concrete formal instance.
- blank: output; 1 bits in the concrete formal instance.
Preserve the original document's public parameters, defaults and symbolic port width expressions. Concrete formal widths do not replace parameterization.
Unspecified declaration order and parameter fields are not established by this binding; retain any explicit qualifications in the answered interface law.

1. Module and ports

The module is named exactly vga_controller. The confirmed named interface is:
Input clock: 1 bit, 25 MHz.
Input reset: 1 bit, active high, asynchronous.
Input color_in: [7:0], RRRGGGBB.
Output hsync: 1 bit.
Output vsync: 1 bit.
Output red: [7:0].
Output green: [7:0].
Output blue: [7:0].
Output next_x: [9:0].
Output next_y: [9:0].
Output sync: 1 bit.
Output clk: 1 bit.
Output blank: 1 bit.

This enumeration is not a verified declaration order. Q22 confirms named bindings but leaves positional declaration order unverified. Named wiring is portable consumer guidance; a consumer possessing the actual module declaration can also instantiate in its declared positional order. This document guarantees no positional order.

The source fixes H_ACTIVE=640, H_FRONT=16, H_PULSE=96, H_BACK=48, V_ACTIVE=480, V_FRONT=10, V_PULSE=2, V_BACK=33. Preserve these exact timing parameter names and values. The source explicitly fixes these values; it establishes no override domain, declaration type, or parameter-dependent port widths. The three inputs and ten outputs above are the entire public boundary. External DACs convert RGB intensities to 0 through 0.7 V and are outside this design.

3. Reset

Reset assertion immediately resets both 10-bit position counters to zero and both phases to ACTIVE, sets internal line-completion bookkeeping to zero, clears red, green, blue, next_x and next_y, and sets hsync, vsync and blank to one. sync is zero. clk follows clock even during reset. Reset has priority over a simultaneous rising clock edge. These reset values hold while reset is high. Repeated clock edges while reset is high consume no pixels. Release alone neither advances positions nor publishes a pixel; display outputs retain the reset values until the next rising edge. This applies equally to initial and mid-frame reset assertions, at either clock level, and does not require reset pulses to overlap a clock edge.

Implementation choice C1: before any reset assertion or rising clock edge, initialize the registered display publication to red=green=blue=next_x=next_y=0 and hsync=vsync=blank=1. sync=0 and clk=clock already follow unconditional source laws. This compatible implementation decision fills only the unspecified initial display-publication scope; it is not a source-established hardware power-up guarantee. Positions zero and phases ACTIVE preserve the source's explicit local initialization. No reset-before-use restriction is imposed. A normal rising edge without any preceding reset publishes the initial pixel and establishes ordinary results. The model's captured color is initialized to zero and valid as part of C1's concrete representation of the initial zero publication, not as an inferred reset of unspecified memory. The initial clock-history baseline is low for the finite scenario; a high first input row represents a rising transition from that baseline.

4. Timing

clock has a 40 ns period at 25 MHz. clk is a continuous direct copy with no registered latency at either edge or during reset. sync is continuously zero. All other outputs are registered publications at each rising edge with reset low, using the pre-edge raster position and color_in at that edge. There is no extra pipeline edge. Falling edges, held clock levels and between-edge color changes retain the published display values. Asynchronous reset overrides those publications immediately.

A line lasts 800 pixel-clock intervals: 640 ACTIVE, 16 front porch, 96 pulse, 48 back porch. hsync has period 800 cycles, LOW for 96 and HIGH for 704. A frame lasts 525 lines or 420000 pixel cycles: 480 ACTIVE lines, 10 front porch lines, 2 pulse lines, 33 back porch lines. vsync has period 420000 cycles, LOW for 1600 and HIGH for 418400. Horizontal timing continues throughout vertical blanking. blank is the AND of the same newly published hsync and vsync; it is HIGH even during porch intervals with neither synchronization pulse active. It does not indicate the complete RGB blanking interval.

Let E0 be reset assertion and let E1, E2, E3 be the first three rising edges after release with reset low. Immediately after E0 and after release, the reset publication holds. E1 publishes pixel (0,0), E2 publishes (1,0), E3 publishes (2,0), each with the color present at that edge. Release consumes no publication edge. For uninterrupted operation, En publishes horizontal position (n-1) modulo 800 and vertical position floor((n-1)/800) modulo 525. The publication is retained for the following completed clock interval. E640 publishes the last active horizontal pixel, E641 publishes the first front-porch pixel, E657 publishes the first horizontal pulse pixel, E753 publishes the first back-porch pixel, E800 publishes the last back-porch pixel, and E801 publishes (0,1). There is no delayed extra line-completion edge. The first frame's final pixel is published at E420000; E420001 publishes (0,0) of the next frame.

The finite model uses explicit clock levels and half-cycle observations. Each row first computes the pending reset or rising-edge transition for that row's inputs and observes its settled outputs, then commits that same transition once. A high row reached from a low row therefore observes the post-rising-edge publication; a physical sample before that rising edge instead sees the preceding publication. Held high rows do not create more rising edges. Rows containing reset assertion immediately observe and commit reset, so release retains the cleared state. The suite is a discrete event scenario, not continuous simulation of unsampled events; the circuit contract still applies to asynchronous pulses between observations. Alternating half-cycle rows at the nominal clock correspond to 20 ns intervals, not 10 ns ticks. iface.json supplies no step_ns or sample_offset_ns value, so this document invents neither observation metadata field.

5. Behaviour

The source's explicitly superseding local experimental completion governs earlier ambiguous coordinate, publication, architecture and waveform shorthand. It is already part of the retained original source, not a new amendment based on an unclear answer. No answer-authorized amendment is introduced.

At every rising edge with reset low, publish all display results from the pre-edge positions, then advance the horizontal position by one modulo 800; when its pre-edge value is 799, advance the vertical position by one modulo 525 on that same edge. Neither position advances at falling edges or reset release. Phase boundaries correspond to those complete position ranges: horizontal ACTIVE 0..639, front porch 640..655, pulse 656..751, back porch 752..799; vertical ACTIVE 0..479, front porch 480..489, pulse 490..491, back porch 492..524. Phase-local 10-bit counters, if represented, reset at their phase boundaries; equivalent observable position representation is expressly allowed by the source. Counters never exceed their 10-bit representation. Internal line-completion bookkeeping indicates completed lines and introduces no additional delay in vertical advancement.

At each publication, hsync is zero exactly for horizontal positions 656 through 751 inclusive and is one otherwise.
At each publication, vsync is zero exactly for vertical positions 490 through 491 inclusive and is one otherwise.
At each publication, blank equals the AND of the hsync and vsync values published on that same edge.
sync is always zero, independently of reset, position and color_in.
clk continuously equals clock, independently of reset, position and color_in.

If both pre-edge positions are ACTIVE, next_x is the horizontal position and next_y is the vertical position; otherwise both outputs are zero.
If both pre-edge positions are ACTIVE, red copies color_in[7:5] into bits [7:5] and has bits [4:0] zero; otherwise red is zero.
If both pre-edge positions are ACTIVE, green copies color_in[4:2] into bits [7:5] and has bits [4:0] zero; otherwise green is zero.
If both pre-edge positions are ACTIVE, blue copies color_in[1:0] into bits [7:6] and has bits [5:0] zero; otherwise blue is zero.

Every 8-bit color encoding is legal. RGB scaling is unsigned concatenation with trailing zeros, producing red and green multiples of 32 and blue multiples of 64; no sign extension or bit replication applies. The model first truncates captured color to eight bits and then performs the unsigned field extraction and scaling; each output truncation is at its declared width. Color affects neither sync timing nor coordinates. The coordinates describe the just-published pixel despite the word next in their names. Publication uses the old positions even though advancement occurs on the same edge. RGB and coordinates are zero throughout either axis's inactive publications. Continuous raster operation has no start, busy, completion port, suppressed first result or terminal halt; subsequent frames repeat indefinitely until reset interrupts them.

6. Worked trace
